  beside them: it is a second water list (Teardrop, Barf Bag, Bubble) and does not match the wet list. A shot Dough copies
  lands as his own, so the soak goes on the shot when it is fired.

### O24 · Event fighters: what step 2 (the lock rule) left to later steps

Step 2 (branch `g-events`) marks an event fighter with `event:true` on its ROSTER row, at the END of the roster, and keeps it off
every list and out of every draw until it is unlocked (`eventLocked`, `isUnlocked`); the Daily and an online room's CPU seats never
take one at all. Checked in scratch copies (Donut moved to the end and marked; a new row appended): nothing changes in the committed
build, and the Daily keeps every pairing. Left on purpose:

- **Every kit job:** its row goes after Bot, below the EVENT FIGHTERS comment in ROSTER, with `event:true`, in the lap's order (J2).
  Its name does NOT go into SHOW_ORDER: the group's place comes from `showRank`.
- **The calendar (build step 3) and the event screens (step 9):** `eventGroupOpen()` is the select screen's hidden switch and is
  `false`. Step 9 turns it on from the first week, writes the locked cells' hint (`unlockHint` has no event line yet; today it
  would say "Keep playing"), and decides whether the board's legend and the "Unlock every fighter" tooltip should mention them.
- **The event match runner (step 7):** the event fighter's own seat is placed by the runner, as the Daily places its foe: no pool
  offers a locked one. Its other CPUs are fighters "you could already have" (B4): no event, Vault or secret fighter, no Four.
- **Tokens (step 4):** the unlock is `unlockFighter(name)`, the same list as every other unlock; nothing else needs to change.
- **Online (step 10):** the EVENT tag in the lobby. A guest's own event fighter already plays in a host's room, and online CPU
  seats already skip them (checked with the in-process pair).
- **Done (the owner, "Hide until launch"; kit 1's fixes):** a computer that cannot save opens the event fighters only once events
  have launched (`eventsLaunched()` in `openProfile` and `isUnlocked`, so a session that crosses the launch Monday opens them too);
  before then they are as hidden there as anywhere.
- **Found in passing, fixed by the launch job (the owner said yes):** once Steve Cobs is beaten, his three prize rows joined ROSTER and with
  it the Daily's pool, so that player's Daily was no longer everyone's (496 of the 508 days from 2026-08-11 to 2027-12-31 differed). Now
  `dailyMatchup` and `oneDailyFoe` leave `r.prize` rows out (see O28).

### O25 · Event fighters: what step 3 (the calendar) left to later steps

Step 3 (branch `g-events`) is the calendar: an event week is the weekly quests' week (`shopWeekNo`, Monday 00:00 UTC to the next
Monday), worked out from the date alone. `EVENT_LAPS` is the append-only table of laps (one row today: the 14 of J2, from lap 1),
`EVENT_LAUNCH` is the first event's Monday and is `null`, so no week is an event week and nothing shows. `eventOfWeek(wk)` gives a
week's event (`week`, `lap`, `n`, `name`, `start`, `end`), `eventThisWeek()` this week's and `eventsComingUp(n)` the next n.
Checked with a test launch (2026-10-19) injected by the check script, not committed: the same 30-week table (two laps and the start
of a third) comes out with the process timezone set to UTC, America/Los_Angeles and Asia/Tokyo, it flips at Monday 00:00 UTC
exactly (1 s and 1 ms before give the week before), a non-Monday or impossible launch date starts nothing, and an appended row
leaves every earlier lap as it was. With the committed `null`, no date from 2000 to 2100 has an event. Left on purpose:

- **The launch (build step 15):** sets `EVENT_LAUNCH` to a Monday at least two weeks after the last kit lands, in a build that
  goes live at least two weeks before it (the announcement). A date that is not a Monday starts nothing.
- **The event match runner (step 7) and tokens (step 4):** the runner keeps `eventThisWeek().week` from the moment its match
  starts, and the payout and the 100-a-week limit use `eventOfWeek(thatWeek)`, so a match that crosses Monday midnight pays toward
  the event it started in.
- **The event screens (step 9):** `eventGroupOpen()` becomes `!!eventThisWeek()` (still `false` today); "ends in 3 days" comes
  from `end`, "Next week" and "Coming up" from `eventsComingUp`, and a locked cell's "back in 6 weeks" from the first week in
  `eventsComingUp(14)` that names it. Before the launch, `eventsComingUp` already lists the first weeks; whether anything shows
  them in the two weeks of announcement is step 9's and 15's call (the plan's announcement is a changelog line).
- **Event rooms (step 14):** the host's week travels as `week`; a guest is paid only if it is the guest's own week or the one
  before.
- **A later phase:** a new row `{ from: lap, order: [...] }` appended to `EVENT_LAPS`, starting at least two weeks after its build
  goes live. No row is ever edited or removed.

### O26 · Event fighters: what step 4 (tokens and saving) left to later steps

Step 4 (branch `g-events`) is question 1's token rule, one for all 14 (J1). `eventPay(m)` pays an event match the moment it ends,
where `m` = { `week` (the week it started in), `n` (1 the duel, 2 their team, 3 their challenge), `won`, `alive`, `dealt`, `secs` },
into the profile's `event` field: `tok`, each fighter's own total (question 2), which only rises and keeps counting past 100
(question 11), and `pay`, the ledger of this week's and last week's payouts. A win pays 10 if you are still in; a loss 5, only with
20% dealt or 60 s lasted; the first win on each match +10, once per event (lines `f1`-`f3`, the same in every tab); no daily limit,
at most 100 a week per fighter; 100 unlocks through `unlockFighter`, for good. The numbers are `EVENT_PAY`. Two tabs: `mergeEvent`
(in `mergeProfiles` and `shopCatchUp`; `eventPay` peeks at the store first, as the quests do) keeps the larger total plus the
payouts only the other side made, inside the week's limit, and a merge that reaches 100 unlocks. A computer that cannot save has the
fighters open once events have launched (step 2, "Hide until launch") and is paid into memory. Nothing calls `eventPay` yet, and with `EVENT_LAUNCH` null it pays nothing.
Checked with a scratch script (test launch 2026-10-19 injected, not committed): 28 payout cases, 6 two-tab merges through
`mergeProfiles` both ways and the game's own `saveProfile`, and the cannot-save path; 49 expectations met. Left on purpose:

- **The event match runner (step 7):** calls `eventPay` once per finished event match, with the week it kept from the start;
  `alive` is "still in at the end", `dealt` the player's total % dealt, `secs` how long the player lasted (to the KO that put them
  out, or to the end). Watched matches pay nothing because the runner does not call it. The runner also keeps event matches out of
  One's chain (question 10: `oneAfterMatch` in `awardMatchProgress`), and asks the owner whether they feed `recordFighterGame`,
  which is both the per-fighter record and the Lightning win rate that opens One's chain. The Daily and the World Cup cannot be
  running during an event match. Quests, stats and My Stats count it as any match.
- **The result note (steps 7 and 9):** reads `eventPay`'s return (`base`, `first`, `bonus`, `weekTotal`, `total`, `unlocked`). An
  unlock is queued in `PENDING_UNLOCKS` like any other, so the existing "new fighter unlocked" note already shows it.
- **Event rooms (step 14):** a guest calls `eventPay` with the host's week; it refuses a week before last week and, since kit 1's
  fixes, a week after this one, which is the "their week or the one just before" rule.
- **For the owner:** a win you are no longer in for (your ally finished it) is paid as a loss is, 5 if you dealt 20% or lasted
  60 s, so being knocked out never pays less because your side went on to win. Paying nothing instead is one line in `eventPay`.

### O27 · Event fighters: what kit 1 (Eraser) left to later steps

Kit 1 (branch `g-events`, build step 6 of event-fighters-phase1.md) is Eraser, the first event row in ROSTER (`event:true`, kit `rubout`),
from section 3.1 and the approved questions 21 to 28: weight 88; run 1.1, jump 1.1, fall 1.03, dmg 1.15 (FIGHTER_STATS; see the balance note below); his wall jump a backflip
15% higher (`WALL_JUMP_HIGH`, the one wall trick of the event cast); BFB+TPOT art only (his TPOT render, already in the game as his assist
picture; HURT_POLY and LIMB_RIG traced from it the trace scripts' way, FR_FACE measured with measure-faces.mjs). His code is the section
EVENT FIGHTERS: ERASER: Rub Out (`eraserRubOut`, `rubOutWipe`; it takes only foes' things a hit could already break, through the object rules,
`eraserTakes`, with `takeKindOf` and `takeSlotOf` beside `canTake`), Steakhouse Flip (`UPSPECIALS.rubout`, `eraserFlip`, the wall kick in
`eraserStep`), Rub It Off (`DOWNSPECIALS.rubout`, `eraserRubOff`), Hard Rub (SMASH_SPEC), Out of the Picture (FINX `ootp`: `finOotp`,
`fxOotpStep`, `ootpFade`, `fxDrawOotp`), Wipe the Slate Clean (`FINAL_SMASHES['Eraser']`), his moveset (`MOVESETS.rubout`, the side tilt and
side special kept as data rows) and his CPU (`aiEraserOut`, `finEventWant`, on `FINX_AI_KITS`). A smash's and a finisher's shots now carry
the move they came from (`_slot`), so no taker ever takes one. Online: everything he draws rides the snapshot (one new flag, `_flipT`; the
finisher's row gains `rn`); the guest's prediction treats Rub Out's air hang and the flip's wall-kick window as frames the host moves him in.
His three event matches are data (`EVENT_MATCHES.Eraser`). Checked: it boots; a scratch-unlocked check of every move and a CPU match; the
in-process pair with him on the guest; one 15-match 1v1 tournament against opponents drawn across the roster (Hard CPUs, 2 stocks, Goiky
Field): 6 of 15, 40%, KOs 12 to falls 20. **The three checks after it, and their fixes** (same branch): the checker's tournament against
mid-pack Hard CPUs (same rules, items off, 7200 frames) went 2 of 15, so he was buffed toward the middle -- the design's knobs first (run
and jump 1.1, Rub Out's cooldown 78 to 60: 1 of 15 on the same seeds), then his damage (dmg 1.1: 4 of 15; with a 48 cooldown and a longer
jab as well: 5 of 15; dmg 1.15: 9 of 15, kept). The builder's roster-spread run again on the final build: 8 of 15 (was 6), KOs 15 to falls 19
(was 12 to 20), damage dealt to taken 0.99 (was 0.85). Both sets together: 17 of 30, 57% (was 8 of 30, 27%). Also fixed: the Final Smash erases every foe's puddle and floor trap (`eraserTakes` with
`all`, canTake's `wide`), as "every enemy shot and trap" says, while Rub Out keeps question 23; Steakhouse Flip from the ground by the keys
takes back the hop the jump key gave (as the up-tilt does), so the kick lands on a standing foe; a 'stall' step of a move ends a fast fall
(down and X pressed together); his CPU presses no Rub Out or Rub It Off with nothing under him, and throws the finisher at a foe within
40 px (`ERASER.ootp.aiGap`); `eventPay` refuses a week after this one; a computer that cannot save opens event fighters only once events
launch. Left on purpose:

- **The event match runner (step 7):** plays `EVENT_MATCHES` as they are written: the event fighter's seat placed by the runner (B4), 2 stocks,
  the items and Smash Balls fixed. The design names no stage for "Pen and Eraser" (and for the other team matches): `stage:null` until the
  runner's job settles it with the owner. "The Trebuchet" is read as a free-for-all of four (the format column) with the usual items and
  Smash Balls (the design names none); both are data, one word each.
- **Twists (step 8):** "The Trebuchet" needs the sponge floor (`twist:'sponge'`); a match whose twist is not built is not played without it.
- **The assist bag:** his trophy stays in the bag until events launch (`ASSIST_LEAVES_AT_LAUNCH`, `assistBagRows`, `eventsLaunched`: false while
  `EVENT_LAUNCH` is null), so nobody loses it before he can be won; the title's assist count follows the bag. Each kit job adds its name.
  Its name tag goes with every assist's in batch A1 (question 22), not here.
- **Recovery:** measured (the rise of the air jump and the up special from a standstill, every fighter): his 283 px is above the roster's median
  (249), so `rec` stays 1. The flip goes backwards, so a person's recovery is steered with the arrows once it is out (his CPU turns its
  back to the floor for the crouch: see the launch job's fixes below).
- **For the owner:** the fighters' standing name pills (every fighter's, not his kit's) still show over him in a match, as over everyone.
- **For the owner (from the checks):** Wipe the Slate Clean's catch is the whole floor, so every foe standing on the floor when it lands is
  held until the eraser hits (21%, 42% with KO-LEVEL, times his dmg 1.15: 48%): "jump it" works only for a foe already in the air or on a platform, as with every floor
  Final Smash. Shrinking the catch is a question for you. Rub It Off's 5 s is the shared special cooldown, so Rub Out and the flip wait too.
  Pencil's down air whiffed when down and X went down together; fixed for everyone by the launch job (below).
- **The launch job's fixes (2026-10-26, same branch).** DOWN AND X TOGETHER (the owner: "fix pencil's down air too"): X is held back
  CI_DELAY frames in case C joins it, so the down pressed with it started a fast fall and the down air landed before its hit. Now
  `comboInput` marks the frames an X or a V is still held back (`_ci.wait`) and `mtFastFall` starts no fast fall on a down pressed then:
  one rule, every fighter. Swept with down and X on the same frame against X a frame first (all 106 playable fighters, the three prize
  fighters installed in memory; a short hop pressed on its frames 10, 14 and 18, drops from 110 and 200 px; a standing dummy under and
  16 px ahead): before, 99 of 106 did less pressed together (Pencil 0% against 8.5% from three of the five starts); after, none. Down
  alone (318 traces) falls frame for frame as before. A CPU's keys are never held back, so its fast falls are untouched (its tournament
  is the same with or without the rule). ERASER: Steakhouse Flip's hop take-back is a person's jump key only (`isHumanCtrl`); his kit
  line says Rub Out's 8% (7 x his dmg 1.15; every other line of his already went through `statWords`); Out of the Picture turns in the
  lean as every finisher does (`fxAimWay`), and his CPU throws it only on a foe it sees standing free on the floor that stays in reach
  through the lean, never just after his own hit (`ootpRead`, `eraserLastHit`), a little more often when that holds (`FIN_AI_EAGER`,
  3); his CPU's recovery turns its back to the floor for the flip's crouch, so the backflip carries him back, and lets go of a C held
  from before so the flip is a new press. Checks: in the re-check's tournament (same seeds) and a second seed set, his finisher went
  from 49 thrown / 9 landed / 18 whiffs (the start commit) to 25 / 11 / 3; from the re-check's 24 off-stage setups x 2 seeds he lost 2
  stocks (was 7; Pen 2). The re-check's "caught 0" was its counter's slip (the lean ends and the grab lands in one step, so the grab's
  start was never seen): the start commit really landed 5 of its 19. Wins against the mid-pack Hard CPUs: the start commit 9 + 1 of 30
  (9 of 15 on the re-check's seeds, 1 of 15 on seed set B0B5EED), this build 3 + 3; with each change taken back alone 3 or 4 of 15 on the
  re-check's seeds, so no one change moved it -- the 9 was the seeds' luck. He sits below the middle on both builds: **his buff is a
  separate pass after launch, checked on at least two seed sets** (the coordinator, 2026-10-27).
- **The launch job's second round (2026-10-27): CPU recovery on par.** The checker found the 2-of-48 held only on the re-check's run order
  (4 seeds: 10 of 96 on both builds, Pen 3, Leafy 4), and most of his losses came after he was back on the stage. Traced, they were:
  (1) SHARED: the recovery pressed jump every other frame on its own say-so (`B.jHeld`), so a jump decided while the CPU's hands were busy
  (`cpuHumanKeys`) was dropped whenever they came free on the frame between, and the CPU fell 10 to 20 frames with its jumps unused; now
  the recovery reads the jump's own latch (`f._jp`): pressed until a jump goes out, let go until the latch clears. (2) SHARED: a press
  decided on the floor and still waiting for the hands came out in the air as an aerial instead of the jump (his down-tilt as Stomp Out
  off a ledge); the hands forget it as a trip off the stage begins (`cpuHandsForget`). (3) SHARED: a CPU with no foe to see (a one-on-one
  just after it KO'd its foe) stood down before the recovery ran and fell after it; it now recovers first. (4) ERASER: no Stomp Out with
  nothing to stand on under where its hang leaves him; Rub It Off only with floor 110 px to both sides (`eraserRoom`); and while more than
  20 frames of the cooldown he shares with the flip are left he steps, runs and drifts no nearer a drop than 110 px (`ERASER.rub.aiEdge`,
  `aiLock`). Checks: the checker's own run (4 seeds, one world per seed, setups back to back): out 50/110 he loses 2 of 96 (was 10; start
  commit 9-10), Pen 1 (was 3), Leafy 1 (was 4); out 170, 2 of 48 (Pen 2, Leafy 1). Order-free (a fresh match and random stream per setup,
  placed inside the world 40/80 px past the floor's end, 8 seeds): Eraser 5 of 192, Pen 5, Leafy 3 (before: 6, 6, 5). Future recovery
  checks should place fighters inside 0..WW (the re-check's "out 110" on Goiky is x -8 or 1032). Balance (not this job's): two fresh seed
  sets 2 + 4 of 15; in-memory knob trials for the buff job, same two sets: dmg 1.25 gave 5 + 1, weight 100 (was 88) gave 7 + 4.

### O28 · Event fighters: Eraser's event goes live (build steps 7-10 for his three matches) -- what it left

The launch job (branch `g-events`, 2026-10-27). The owner, 2026-10-26: "add him as an event immediately" (over J2's wait for all 14), "Eraser
stays until Spongy", "Yes, remove it now" (his assist), and yes to keeping Steve Cobs's prize rows out of the Daily.

- **The launch.** `EVENT_LAUNCH = '2026-10-26'` (Monday 00:00 UTC). The lap runs only through the kits that are BUILT (`eventLapOrder`: a lap
  row's order with every name that has no event row in ROSTER left out), so every week is Eraser's until Spongy's row exists, and each kit that
  lands joins in the plan order. Still the date and the build alone. His assist left the bag with the launch (`assistBagRows`, already gated).
  The select screen's Event fighters group is on from the launch (`eventGroupOpen` = `eventsLaunched`); a locked cell's hint is `eventHint`
  ("His event is on this week: 40 of 100 tokens", or "Back in N weeks (40 saved)").
- **The runner (step 7).** `EVENT_PICK` (the match picked on the event page, held through the select screen, the match and its result),
  `startEventMatch` (the fixed setup: stage, lineup, sides, items, Smash Balls, twist, 2 stocks, Normal map size; a side of more than one makes
  it Teams), `eventLineup` (you first, then the CPUs side by side, your side team 0; a CPU One has erased gets `oneDailyFoe`'s stand-in, the
  event fighter never), `eventFinish` (checkWin: `eventPay` with the week the match started in; alive, % dealt, seconds lasted to the KO that
  put you out: `f._outT`), `returnEventLoan` (mode, contestants, stocks, team split, items, stage, map size, Smash Balls), handed back when the
  player LEAVES the match or its result screen (go), so the result reads the match it shows and Rematch replays it. Counts for quests, stats
  and My Stats; not One's chain (no `oneAfterMatch` and no `recordFighterGame`, whose Lightning record opens her chain), the Daily or the
  World Cup. RIVAL: event CPUs count (they did KO you), but `rivalPick` never names a locked event fighter (or a prize fighter before his gate):
  the next one down is named until he is yours.
- **Pen and Eraser's stage** (the design names none; since O29 the match is The Alliance, a 3 vs 1): Stone Pillars. A team match is Teams, which always plays on the Teams arena with the
  stage's sky and ground; Stone Pillars has no hazard and is not the duel's stage. One word in `EVENT_MATCHES` if the owner wants another.
- **The twists (step 8, his two).** Food only (`eventItemPool`: the healing snacks only, the usual rate and weights, one draw as before).
  The sponge floor (`MATCH_TWIST`, `SPONGE`, `spongeLanding`, `spongeBounce`, `drawSpongeFloor`): only a HARD landing on the main floor bounces
  -- 21 px a frame or more for a free fighter (above any fighter's own double jump, about 18-20: a long drop), 10 for a tumbling one (in hitstun)
  -- at 0.6 of the speed (8 to 15), with the jumps back; a fighter in a move lands as on any floor (no move loses its landing hit). Art (a
  yellow sponge band with pores under a green scrubber) and the jump sound, no words. Online: the guest's prediction (`netPredRun`) bounces by
  the same rule; nothing new rides the relay (no room can have a twist yet: event rooms are step 14, whose start message would carry it).
- **The screens (step 9).** The title's event card beside the Daily card (`refreshEventCard`; its button inside the card, so the title keeps
  its six), the event page (`#event`, `buildEventPage`: who and his pronouns, "ends in N days", the token bar, the week's limit and the rules in a
  sentence, the three match cards with the twist in plain words, a first-win tick and Play, his move list, "Next week"), the select screen's
  strip for an event match (`syncEventSelect`: the fixed setup and the CPU skill where the settings were; Play; Esc or Back to the event page),
  and the result note (`showEventNote`) with Rematch and "Event ▸". Keyboard: all buttons; Esc goes back from the page and the pick.
- **Online (step 10).** The lobby's EVENT tag on a pick that is an event fighter this computer has not unlocked (`lobbyRow`). Online CPU seats
  already never take one; a guest's own Eraser plays in any host's room.
- **The Daily:** `dailyMatchup` and `oneDailyFoe` leave `r.prize` rows out (O24's note).

**Fixes after the two checkers (2026-10-28):** the event note and "Event ▸" are hidden whenever the result screen is left (`go` ->
`hideEventNote`), so Boss Rush, One's and Steve Cobs's fights, RUNNING! and a guest's Boss Rush never show an old note; the event pick keeps its
labels after the CPU-skill click or any `buildBoard` (`cupPickSync` ends by re-applying `syncEventSelect` while `EVENT_PICK` is held); the arrow
keys pick a fighter and Enter presses Play on the event pick (`selGridKey`, as in the World Cup pick); an erased CPU's stand-in is never a
Vault fighter (`eventStandIn`: `oneDailyFoe` redrawn with the next seed, `oneDailyFoe` itself untouched so the Daily keeps its draws); the
lobby EVENT tag's tooltip uses the fighter's pronouns (`eventInfo`); wording: the team match reads "Teams arena (Stone Pillars sky)", the
duel's "real edges" shows in the strip, "Wins on Easy count the same", the rules in short sentences (no "makes him yours" once he is), "Won
this week: N of 100 (the weekly limit)", no bonus line over the limit, and a team win after you were out says it pays as a loss.
Open for the owner: the sponge's "hard landing" line (`SPONGE.hard = 21`): no fighter's own jump and double jump bounces (highest Baseball 20.47
px a frame), but a fast fall from the top of a double jump lands at 19.0-24.8 depending on the fall stat, so it bounces some fighters and not others.

FFA and Teams) clean. All three matches ran to the end CPU against CPU (50 s, 259 s, 122 s of game) and with a scripted player (32 s, 259 s,

### O29 · Event fighters: the team match is a 3 vs 1 (every event fighter's match 2) -- what it left

Branch `g-event-3v1` (from `ii-dlc` `1837133`, 2026-10-28). The owner: "event fighters should actually have 3v1, which would give you bonus
tokens to win it"; asked, he chose "You alone vs 3", "Replace the team match" and, for the bonus, "Both" (a bigger win and a bigger first win).

- **The rule, for every event fighter (`EVENT_MATCHES` header):** match 2, their team, is YOU ALONE against three CPUs -- the event fighter and
  two of their team -- kind `'3v1'`, sides `[['you'], [name, a, b]]`, 3 stocks for you (`youStocks`, set in `buildFighters` for the player's
  seat only, offline only) and 2 for each of them, the CPU skill the player picks (Easy counts the same), on the Teams arena (a side of more
  than one is Teams: team split 1v3, the stage giving the sky and ground). Everything else fixed as before; the settings loan hands it all back.
- **Eraser's: The Alliance** (was "Pen and Eraser": Eraser + Pen vs you + Blocky). The same cast, with Blocky gone over to them: you vs Eraser,
  Pen and Blocky -- in BFDI those three were the alliance. Food only kept ("gimme gimme food"); the stage kept: the Teams arena in Stone Pillars'
  sky (no hazard, and the arena's room lets one fighter split the three up and get to the food; a small stage would be a swarm).
- **Stocks: 3 against 2 each,** as briefed. Nothing in the code argues for other numbers: no fighter count or stock rule caps it, DWS already
  scores a fighter by the stocks they started with (`s0` counts stocks left plus falls), the HUD draws each fighter's own, and Rematch keeps it.
- **Pay (`EVENT_PAY_3V1 = { win:20, first:30 }`, `eventRate(m)`):** a win on the 3 vs 1 pays 20 (10 elsewhere), its first win each week 30 more
  (10 elsewhere); a loss pays as any (5 if you dealt 20% or lasted a minute, else 0); all of it within the week's 100 and toward the unlock at
  100 (the bonus is cut to what the 100 leaves, as before). With the three first wins a keen week is now 20 + 20 + 50 = 90 from one win of
  each, so the unlock takes four wins (was about seven).
- **Screens:** the card reads "🍎 His team, 3 vs 1 · The Alliance · Teams arena (Stone Pillars sky) · you vs Eraser, Pen and Blocky · 3 stocks for
  you, 2 each for them · food only", the twist line, and "Win: 20 tokens · first win this week: +30" (taken: "Win: 20 tokens · ✓ first win this
  week: bonus taken"; the other two cards keep their line); the rules: "A win pays 10 Eraser tokens if you are still in at the end, and 20 on The
  Alliance, where you face three alone. ... Your first win on each match pays 10 more (30 on The Alliance)."; the select strip adds the same pay
  line; the result note: "+20 Eraser tokens for beating all three · first win on The Alliance +30 · 50/100". Event CPUs still appear only in their
  own matches (Pen and Blocky are regular fighters). The duel and The Trebuchet are unchanged.

**Checks (2026-10-28; jsdom only, at most three node processes at BelowNormal, each with a timeout; no browser, no relay):** the syntax check
passes (2 scripts); boot (CPU FFA and Teams) clean. The 3 vs 1 through the real screens (event page, Play, the strip, Play), lineup "Pencil/T0/3
stocks (you), Eraser, Pen, Blocky/T1/2 stocks each", Teams 1v3 on the Teams arena, food only (only heals and marmalade dropped): CPU against CPU
to the end twice (the lone CPU lost both: 434 s with 0 KOs, 554 s with 3 of the 6), paid a loss that fought 5; with a scripted player twice (it
won both, 6 KOs and no falls, 307 s and 259 s, on Normal), paid 20 + 30 with the note "+20 Eraser tokens for beating all three · first win on
The Alliance +30 · 50/100"; every setting handed back exactly each time. Pay forced through the real KO path (last stocks knocked past the blast
zone; checkWin, eventFinish, eventPay): a first win 20 + 30, a second win 20, a loss after lasting a minute 5, a quick loss 0, 60 held this week

### O30 · Balance pass (2026-10-28): the jab fixes, Bomby's mine, the bottom-20 buffs  [BUILT; re-run done, the plan's fallback applied and re-measured]

Branch `g-balance` (from `ii-dlc` `4b3397f`). The plan is `smash-bal/reports/balance-pass-plan.md` (evidence: `jab-study.md`, `bottom-fighters.md`).
The owner: "Build it all", "Yes, stop the jab lock", and Tea Kettle gets the reach-8 jab too. No CPU changes; Bow's kit is untouched (only the shared jab lock reaches her).

- **Empowered jab:** Firey's keeps its damage bonus and loses its launch bonus (launch 13 -> 7); Magnet's launches x1.3 instead of x1.6 +2 (17 -> 12).
  Trophy, David, Cake, Fan, Blueberry and every other banked empower are unchanged. Recovering CPU, mid-stage / ledge: Firey 87 / 21 -> 142 / 83,
  Magnet 44 / 0 -> 83 / 17.
- **The jab lock (every fighter):** a jab cannot combo into your own jab. Hold X and walk: 2 to 17 linked jabs -> 1. A jab still combos into other moves
  (jab -> down-tilt linked for the same 14 of 19 fighters before and after, jab -> special for the same 3; jab -> jab 15 -> 0).
- **Bomby:** one Proximity Mine out at a time (a new one removes the old); damage 20 / launch 12 unchanged. Up to 7 were out at once.
- **Jab reach 8** (damage and launch unchanged): new rows for Goo, Lifering, Yin-Yang, Starfruit, Cammy, Trophy, Clover, Candle and Tea Kettle; Box and
  Tissues 2 -> 8; Snowball 2 -> 6. The default jab stays for Fan, Blueberry and Jack.
- **Per fighter:** Goo's Drip From Above 8%; Box dmg 1.15; Lifering's Spare Ring 6%; Balloon weight 66; Snowball run 1.0; Teardrop's THE KICK cooldown

It still never goes through a wall, but now goes round, along the wall's face or along the block over or under it. Before it sets off it looks ahead (its own rules, its foe held still, about its whole life) and takes whichever way gets it on sooner; on a tie, up the face and over (down it when its spot is under the wall's bottom) or to the block's nearer end. Where no way round gets it anywhere (a wall to the top of the world, a closed door, a foe shut in a room, an overhang), or it is turned back at both ends, it holds still as before until its foe moves on. Before that rule a person holding left walked off the edge at x -76 to -92, off the screen, and lost a stock 7 frames later (the check of this branch). The centre chamber's rung is 9 px lower (head 2 px clear of the arms' ceiling; was 7 px into it) and the unstick burst taps jump instead of holding it. - **2026-10-30, a jump back for a hit (the owner: "you should get a jump back when you have no jumps left while in the air and you connect a hit"; every hit, not once an airtime: "you should be rewarded for air control"; "your own body/weapon hits"; branch `g-airjump`):** in the air with no jumps left, each hit of your own body or what you hold (an aerial, tilt, special, smash, throw or finisher: `ownHit`, the hit HIT FREEZE holds the attacker for) on a foe, a boss or a boss's add gives ONE jump back (`hitJumpBack`); never a shot, trap, puddle, tick, assist, summon or item, and no hit means no jump, so nobody stalls off the stage. How high it goes is a per-fighter knob, `HIT_JUMP_K` (a share of their own air jump; 0 = none). Measured in jsdom: at 1 for everyone, 94 of 106 fighters could chain an up-air ladder or a neutral-air string to a KO; each got the highest share with no loop at it and the next one down. A re-run with a CPU foe lowered six more (Magnet 0.08; Bell, Dora, Fanny 0.15; Pin 0.35; Tea Kettle 0.5). The online guest's prediction knows the lower jump (`netPredOut` `hj`). Checks: syntax, boot, a CPU FFA and Teams match and a Moon Caves Teams match with no errors; alone off the stage swinging gives nothing back; hitting a recovering foe does.
- **2026-10-30, at most 2 jumps back an airtime (the owner: "restrict max air jumps to 3", then "no, the max of extra air jumps should be 2"):** `HIT_JUMP_MAX` 2: hits give back at most two jumps in one airtime, on top of the fighter's own (`f._hjN`, counted in `hitJumpBack`, reset on landing). Probe: own jumps used, two hits each give one back, the third gives none; landing resets. Syntax, boot, CPU FFA and Teams clean.
- **2026-10-31, the Plays Camera shows plays as they happen (the owner: "the \"plays\" camera shows me plays AFTER theyre done"):** a play is only known once it has happened -- a string at its third move, a finisher once its damage is in, a KO at the blow, a recovery half a second in -- and the camera cut when it heard of one (after a dwell or hold of up to 1.5 s more), so it always arrived after it. LOOK-AHEAD: every match of the session is now played twice on the same dice, the fixture's own (`fx._pcSeed`) from the same clean slate: a scout copy nobody sees runs `PCAM_LOOK` (240) of the match's own frames ahead of the copy the camera shows, and the plays are read off the scout with when they start and their key moment (a KO's blow, a string's or a finisher's first hit, a root landing, a trip's start). The director cuts `PCAM_LEAD` (60) frames before a play starts and never later than `PCAM_SEE` (20) frames before its key moment; a play it cannot get to in time is let go, never shown late. A match nobody sees starts once its scout has its lead; the match on screen holds the camera while a play of its own is coming or playing out; KOs still skip the dwell. Every 30 frames the shown copy checks it is where its scout was (`pcCheck`); if they ever part, that match goes on without look-ahead (plays caught as they happen, as before). K taking over a watched fixture (`pcAdopt`) has no scout for that one match. Measured in jsdom (`pcFrame` per frame, spectated 16-team cup with the camera on): today's build, 1v1 seed 7 -- 15 cuts for plays, at a median 0 frames after the key moment (KOs during the flight, roots as they land), recoveries 30-51 frames late, none before; this build, the same round -- every cut before the play (12/12), at a median 60 frames before it starts; 1v1 seed 3 18/18, a K takeover 15/15, a 2v2 round 17/17, all at least 23 frames before the key moment. The two copies never parted in any run, and the cup results are the same as today's build's (seed 7: all 8 fixtures). Cost: about twice the simulation per match; in 1v1 rounds the camera still keeps 4 matches behind the one on screen, in a 2v2 round `pcPace` keeps one there instead of two or three (the round takes longer; the camera shows fewer plays, each from its start).
- **2026-10-31, Moon Caves M2: the real map and its KOs (the owner: "build them. hide it."; plan `smash-bal/reports/moon-caves-design.md`, the owner's copy; still behind `MCAVE_DEV`):** M1's fixed test disc is replaced by the plan's generator (`mcGen`, section 3): PURE (no `Math.random`) and mirror-symmetric about the 12-6 line, sized from the number of fighters and the map size -- the surface at 620*sqrt(N) (1700 to 3400) times the map size, the crust 140 deep, caves 560 apart (420 px of headroom) down to an innermost floor of r 800 or more (at most 4), the shell over the hollow core, the core KO at the shell's underside - 200 and the space KO at the surface + 1300. Holes 220 wide in every band but the shell, one per ~1300 px of arc in the crust and each band below in the middle between the holes above (so routes zigzag and no hole is under the steps above); under each, one-way steps at +140, +280 and +420 (in the hole's mouth), 140 below the floor above (every climb 140 px); a ledge 210 px over each cave floor between the holes above; hop-tops 180/260 px over the surface. Cracks 260 wide in the shell at 12 and 6 o'clock (from 16 players also 1:30, 4:30, 7:30, 10:30), no plank over one and no hole straight above one. The safe strips are world rectangles 700 px tall through the middle (team 0 at 9 o'clock), from the shell's underside out past the blast ring; each has pads on every floor, its lift (a hole through every band but the shell, its steps pads too) and three hop-tops: 13 pads a side at 2-4 fighters, 20 at 10, 27 at 16-20, 34 at 30 (the plan's minimum, 1.2 x a team, is 18 at 15 a side). Zone tests (`enemyZoneOf`, `inOwnTeamZone`, the reform's crowd check in `pickSpawnPad`) read the real place inside a frame (`mcRealPt`); Moon Caves is always two sides (`teamSplitsFor`, `buildFighters`); Marshmallow's gravity saves her just short of the core; the slow-mo's cue flies the launch in real space with the pull toward the middle (`mcKoFlight`: past the blast ring or into the core is a KO, into rock it is stopped) and its close-up is at the real place. Checks (throwaway, scratchpad): the generator at 2/4/10/16/20/30 fighters and Compact/Huge -- every climb 140 px, no plank over a crack, no hole above one, every pad on a floor and in its strip, every piece and pad mirrored, none in the strips but the lifts: 0 failures; 4/10/20-CPU matches, 1800 steps: no errors or NaN, everyone starts in their own strip, a reform lands back in it; by hand: a hard hit off the surface KOs into space (the cue says so), a weak one does not, the same launch in a cave bonks on the ceiling (cue: no), a spike through a crack is a core KO, beside one the floor holds, Marshmallow is saved once; the same-game replay (FFA on 4 stages, Teams 2v2/4v4/2v2v2/10v10, CTF, two World Cup fixtures; 1800 steps hashed every step) identical to the build before. At 16+ fighters the band over the shell gets no contested holes (six cracks and the strips leave no room under the rules), so the deepest cave is reached by the lifts. Pictures: `moon-caves-10.png`, `moon-caves-30.png`. Next: M3 (every move, shot and item on the disc). The CPUs still circle the ring the same way as their foes (M5).
- **2026-10-31, Moon Caves M3-M4, and M5 begun: every move, shot and item on the disc; the full view; the CPUs (the owner: "do the moon caves job"; plan `moon-caves-design.md`; still behind `MCAVE_DEV`, nothing shipped):** **M3.** The frames chart the kit-made platforms (`FX_PLATS`, `FILE_RIDES`, `SPONGY_PLATS`) and the Smash Ball; the remembered points (`MC_PAIRS`, `MC_SUBS`: a drop's shadow, an arm's start, a cast, a summon's aim and start, a split's anchor, a Final Smash's pin and its spots `fsAx/fsAy`, `fsBx/fsBy`, `fsCx/fsCy`, Lightning's chain `_fs.px/py`, a crush's and a pull's mark, a shot's launch origin) are kept real and charted in and out exactly as positions; the marks a frame leaves (hit art, impact dust/debris/scars, KO bursts, the hitbox view, beams as `{r, a0, a1}` arcs) are turned to real places as it closes; shots die in the core, far in space or half a lap round; items spawn on floors outside the strips and grow in over 12 frames. A 10-CPU match with items, 3600 steps: no errors, NaN or jumps; every assist summoned in a cave and on the surface stays on its floor. **M4.** Everything drawn round its own spot stands upright there (`mcUpright`: shots and their reach, items (grown in), the Smash Ball, summons, file rides, kit effects, statuses, stance art, the Final Smash's aura and effects, a launch out of a mouth); a finisher's record is drawn in its own chart (`mcFinxDraw`); beams are arcs; scars and dust lie along their floor; name tags read screen-up; the Final Smash close-up, `fsToScreen` and `viewRect` go through the turned view. The disc is culled to the view; craters in the crust's face and rubble in the cave floors' faces, fixed at build from the pieces (`mcDeco`, a hash, no dice); Earth fixed in space, shown only while it is up in the sky. The strips are a tint and a flag on the surface, no line and no words. A disc minimap (north up, rings, strips, dots, the turned view box) and the edge marks through `worldToScreen`. THE WATCHING VIEW (CPU-only or once you are out): the camera follows the middle of the closest pair of foes, held about 3 s on a pair; Z shows the whole disc north up, eased in and out. Pictures (cloud container, headless Chromium): 12, 4:30, 7:30, 9 o'clock, a cave, the shell, a Final Smash, the minimap and edge marks, the watching view and the whole disc. **M5 (begun).** The unstick check measured `f.x`, which a CPU in its own chart never changes: every CPU was "stuck" 11% of the time (now by where it really is); the flankers' high road is the surface, so a flanker on it no longer climbs at space (it jumped at nothing 30% of the time); a far foe round the ring is gone after the way the team's even slots go over 12 o'clock and the odd under 6, chosen once a foe (both teams took the same way round and chased each other's backs); under the shell the way back is up through the nearest crack; the pit sense is on (drops only over something, never into a crack); out of its strip toward its foe; about five on the team plan's focus. The climb out of a cave went by `steerClimb` toward the floor above, and `navUp` takes the nearest step in reach anywhere: beside a hole next to its own strip that was the strip lift's, the route pulled back to the hole, and the CPU swung between the two where one hands over to the other (45 s on one step, 20 a side, out of the shell). NOT MET, told to the owner: "no snap over 160 px". The physics being the host's, it is the online input timing (a key is played the step it arrives), shared by every map: a change there is the owner's call.

The mode buttons rebuild it now (`buildStages`; its `stageGuard` puts a stage the new mode does not offer back to the first). Clicked through in the booted game, no dev switch, One beaten: FFA lists The Moon; Teams lists The Moon and Moon Caves, and picking it offers 30; back to FFA, Moon Caves is gone and the stage is Goiky Field (the live build before this: Teams listed The Moon only).
- **2026-10-31, NOT A NUMBER: the site's crash in Bow's Teleport glow, and why it could not go away on Moon Caves (the owner's screenshot: "ERROR: TypeError: The provided double value is non-finite. | at drawTeleportFx", on the site):** the line is the glow's `createRadialGradient` at her place, which throws only when that place is no number (NaN or Infinity) -- Chrome's gradients throw at one where an `arc` just draws nothing. About 950 teleports did not make one (CPU Bows on nine maps, a human Bow on random keys through the real draw path with a canvas that throws as Chrome's does, a guest Bow online in the in-memory host and guest pair, and long varied CPU matches on Moon Caves with the first mover at no place looked for), so its first cause is not known. What was found is why one bad number is fatal on Moon Caves and nowhere else: put a Bow at NaN on Goiky Field and she is KO'd and comes back, as anyone past a blast line; put one at NaN on Moon Caves and the next step turns EVERY fighter, shot and item to NaN for good. A frame opened round a mover at no place has a NaN chart, and when it closes, "untouched" is `x === its chart x` -- NaN equals nothing -- so every object charted in it is written back as NaN, and nothing at no place is ever past the blast ring or in the core. Fixed: (1) `mcEnter` opens no frame round a mover at no place (its own step runs unframed and touches nothing); (2) `nanGuard`, at the end of every step: a living fighter whose place or speed is no number goes back to where it last was a number, standing still, its other NaN numbers cleared, a shot or an item at no place is gone, a summon goes back to its last place; it notes what it found in `window.__nanLog` and once a match in the console, so the next one can be traced; (3) the CPUs' late sight (`cpuSeeSwap`) writes no number it saw as no number onto a body -- it kept putting the repaired Bow back at NaN for as long as they saw late; (4) nothing of a kit's effects is drawn at no place, and the glow checks its own numbers; (5) a guest's own fighter: an offset that is no number is cut (NaN failed the size test, so it never eased away or cut), and a prediction that comes out no number is drawn where the glide has it. Checked: the NaN Bow on Goiky and on Moon Caves (live main: the picture throws and on Moon Caves every fighter is NaN for good; now: no throw, she is back where she was, the others never touched, nothing comes back in 300 steps), a NaN shot (gone, nobody touched). A fighter that never goes wrong is never touched.
- **2026-10-31, the grab window, more lenient (the owner: "make the window to use grab more lenient"):** X + C "together": a plain X on the ground (no arrow: the standing jab) waits 8 frames for C (`CI_GRAB_DELAY`, was 5), and the window now runs from the X's press whether X is still down or not -- a quick tap let go before C lands is still half of the pair, and its jab goes out at the end of the window if no C comes (it went out the moment the tap was let go). C first counts let go too: a tapped neutral C, still waiting for a direction (`SPECIAL_DIR_WINDOW`), takes an X pressed in that wait. And a C that was half of a grab is never a special as well (X let go first with C still down fired one, 8 frames after the grab). The special's own timing is untouched (a C with an arrow goes at once; a neutral C waits its 5 frames as before), and so are an aimed jab, the tilts and the aerials (2 frames). Players only: the CPUs press the grab themselves. The price: the standing jab comes out on frame 7 after the press (it was 4). Measured, live main -> this build, on a human fighter: X held and C 6 frames later, a jab -> the grab; an X tapped for 2 frames and C 4 or 6 later, a jab and a special -> the grab; C tapped and X 3 later, a special and a jab -> the grab; X held and C 9 later, the jab both times; X + C with X let go first, the grab and a special -> the grab alone.
- **2026-10-31, CPUs wear skins (the owner: "cpus should be allowed to use skins"):** it was "the AI wears nothing: no looks are ever rolled for it". Now a CPU wears one of its own fighter's skins about half the time (`CPU_LOOK.share`): any of them, bought or not (the wallet is the player's), a seasonal one only in its month (`cosInSeason`), never one a player in the match wears on the same fighter (two Rockstar Poppys could not be told apart), and only the skin -- no hat, trail or KO effect. The pick is a hash of the match's look seed, the fighter and its slot (`cpuCos`): a new seed each match from the clock, never the match's dice, so a replay, a scout copy and the CPUs' own play are the same with or without it; online the host's seed travels in the start (`look`) and every screen dresses each CPU the same (players keep their own looks, by the start's codes). Looks only: every reader of `cosOf` draws. Checked: 40 local matches (160 CPUs, all of fighters with skins): 87 wore one (54%), 65 different skins, every one its own fighter's, the player's Rockstar Poppy never copied onto a CPU Poppy, the same seed the same looks, nothing seasonal out of its month; online (the in-memory host and guest pair, 8 and 10 fighters): host and guest dress every fighter the same; a picture in headless Chromium: Rockstar Poppy, Broken Fries and Robot Pin as CPUs.
- **2026-10-31, Moon Caves: every layer its own platforming, normal gravity below, the deepest a furnace (the owner: "make the platforming different across the layers of the moon; the lower layers should have normal gravity, and the lowest ones an effect like the incinerator. by different, i mean different apart from these stage conditions"):** THE LAYERS are the surface and each cave (`mcGen`'s `layers`, `mcLayerOf`): 2-4 a side two, 5-7 three, 8-12 four, 13-15 five (Normal size). The surface is as it was (hop-tops). Each cave has a kind, from the top (`G.caveKinds`; the deepest is always the furnace), laid on the arcs its passages leave free (`freeRuns`: clear of the steps under the holes above, the holes in its floor, the strips and the cracks), each row centred on its run, so the two halves still match exactly (every piece has its mirror image, every size): the GALLERIES (the first cave, with two caves or more) keep the ledge over each hole, with a top tier 330 px up over it, and rows of one-way ledges 140 and 280 px up in turn between the passages (pale blue, cold walls): up in the air; the COLUMNS (the next, three or more) have no ledges -- rock up from the floor (70 / 50 px, hopped) and rock hanging from the roof down to 250 px, in turn, and a drip over each hole in the floor (brown): on the ground, round the rock; the TUNNELS (the next, four) bring the roof down to 160 px over the floor between the passages (a full jump bonks; dark); the FURNACE (always the deepest) has low one-way stepping stones 110 px up instead of ledges, basalt and embers, and a floor that flares as The Incinerator's -- 70 frames in every 260 on its clock, 0.5% a frame and a pop every 6 to whoever stands on it then, Firey and Firey Jr. untouched -- except in the strips (a reform comes out on their pads), and within 220 px of a crack it burns once every 6 frames and never pops (`mcHotFloor`); its glow is the Incinerator's colour and 10 px. GRAVITY: the upper half of the layers keeps the Moon's low gravity, the lower half is normal (2-7 a side only the surface is low, 8-15 the surface and the galleries), by a body's middle (a layer runs from the underside of its floor to the underside of the floor above), in step() (`gravFor`), the guest's own prediction (`netPredRun`, step by step) and everything that predicts a fall or a jump (`gravAt`: the CPUs' reads of a recovery, a landing, a jump into a danger and how high they climb, a smash hop, a fling, the finishers' holds `fxGrav`, the KO flight) -- which is also the design's risk 8 for The Moon and Yoyleland, whose predictions used GRAV. The cracks, the hollow core and the furnace's air over each crack (a faint heat column, the core's heat rising) keep the Moon's low gravity: they are no layer anyone fights on. Under a hole into a cave of normal gravity the steps are four, 112 px apart (a jump rises 120 there; 140 took both jumps), the low-gravity caves keep three. WHAT THE CPU PROBE FOUND, and what fixed it (CPU-only 2, 5 and 10 a side, 3 minutes, items, two seeds): at first, at 5 a side, 10 of 13 KOs were falls into the core and 5 of them with no hit before them (live main: 1 of 11). The causes, each traced frame by frame: the flare's pop threw a runner over a crack's edge (now no pop near a crack, and a pop halves the run's speed); CPUs on a route walked into a crack, or back into a hole they had just climbed out of (now a gap at their own level is jumped on a route, `mcGapHop`, but the hole a route down is going for); a CPU coming down over a crack only began to recover once under the shell, falling 15-20 px a frame by then (now it heads for the lip at once, `mcCrackGoal`); CPUs climbing the steps beside a crack spent both jumps and fell in with none (the heat columns, and the one-jump steps); and in the air a route's second jump and up-special went for a step anywhere, so every hop over the columns' rock became a double jump at the roof (now only for a step in reach). A weighting of the slow caves in the routes was tried and taken out (no better). After: quiet falls into the core 0 in all four runs at 5 and 10 a side (live main 2 in the same four), nobody stuck, the CPUs as often near a foe at 5 a side (0.60 / 0.64 of the time; live 0.61 / 0.63) and a little less at 10 (0.49 / 0.51; live 0.59 / 0.57). THE PRICE, for the owner's play to judge: about 30% fewer KOs in 3 minutes at 5 and 10 a side (33 against live main's 47 over the four runs) -- the CPUs spend more of the match underground, where the caves' roofs stop a launch -- and a climb from the deepest cave to the surface at 10 a side takes 22-58 s (live main 10-23; at 5 a side 13-22, as live). THE GATES: the replay of the 11 recorded matches on the other maps, 10 identical, and Yoyleland's 5-player parts at step 872, as meant (its low gravity now reaches the CPUs' and the moves' predictions: risk 8). Online, a host and a guest at 5 a side in three spots: the guest builds the same disc (the pieces' hash), no errors, and its corrections are main's or smaller (mean 3.4 / 7.0 / 5.4 px at 12 o'clock, 3 o'clock and in a cave, main 3.4 / 7.4 / 6.5; three over 160 px at 3 o'clock as on main, each glided: none moved 80 px in a frame). SPEED, the other price: a step at 30 CPUs is about 16% slower (jsdom, two runs each in turn: 27.1 / 27.9 ms against main's 22.5 / 24.8; the 95th percentile 43 against 34-37) -- the layers' pieces are 296 at 30 fighters against 228, and every frame reads all of them in its arc. A cut by depth (a frame given only the pieces near its mover's depth) was tried and taken out: the same seeded matches with and without it parted (both ways at 800 px, 14 frames in: a CPU over two holes in line saw a pit; 800 px up only, 22 s in: a lemon laid 990 px over its owner). Pictures (headless Chromium): each layer at 15 a side, the furnace mid-flare.
- **2026-11-01, finishers' art on the moon stays where it was left, along the floor (the owner's screenshot: Sidewalky's crack hanging tilted in a cave's air; "bug with traps/ finishers like sidewalky's on the moon"):** a finisher's record keeps its points in the chart it was made in (`mcAnchor`), and `mcFinxDraw` set the canvas so that chart landed on the world at the record's OWNER, turned by the owner's angle. Exact while the owner stands where it made it; but what a finisher leaves behind -- the crack, Match's fuse, the press's belt, the disco floor, a patch, a griddle, a campfire -- went wherever the owner went, laid along the owner's tangent there (in the air, at a tilt, or off the screen); and even with the owner beside it a 400 px crack, drawn straight, rose off the curved floor -- 45 px at its far end on the surface at 2 a side, 70 and more in the deepest caves. Now the canvas is pinned at THE PIN: of the record's own place (its chart's middle) and its owner now, the point between them nearest the view's middle -- the same point as before while the owner has not moved, and the record's own place when the view is on what it left. Long art on the floor is bent onto the curve: the crack's and the fuse's points (`mcBendPt`: each point moved so the pinned canvas draws it at its true place), the belt's edges, rollers and arrows, and the press and each disco tile pinned where they are (`mcRepin`). Drawing only: the records' play is unchanged (it always ran in the exact chart). Pictures (headless Chromium): the crack on the surface at 2 a side with its owner beside it and gone, and a crack, a press and a disco floor in the deepest cave at 5 a side -- before, the crack rose off the floor and vanished with its owner; after, it lies along the floor and stays.
- **2026-11-01, Moon Caves: deeper is tunnels -- two tunnels one over the other, with Capture the Flag's slopes (the owner: "tunnels should have layers of tunnels. as you go deeper, the concept of caves should become tunnels, with slopes as well" -- "use the slopes from ctf"):** THE ORDER: the cave over the furnace is always the tunnels, from two caves (5 a side and up); the galleries are the first cave from three (8 and up), the columns the next with four (13-15). So 2-4 a side surface / furnace (as it was), 5-7 surface / tunnels / furnace (the galleries were there), 8-12 surface / galleries / tunnels / furnace (the columns were there), 13-15 all five. THE TUNNELS: between two shafts up, two tunnels one over the other -- the lower one 200 px high, a divider 50 px thick, the upper one up to the roof (170 px) -- the upper one's floor rising over a hill of Capture the Flag's slopes (`'slope'` pieces: steps 22 px high and 40 wide, walked up without a jump: the boxes are `ramp`, the fighter step's ramp step-up), and in a run 900 px and longer two hills, with a bump of the same steps on the lower one's floor between them. The steps under a hole into the tunnels zigzag in a NARROW SHAFT (`stepsT`: 110 px steps either side of the middle in turn, 100 px each way, the four heights as before), so the two tunnels run up to them -- the lower one's floor under them, the upper one's floor 26 px over the second. The tunnels' floor keeps every other hole down (in mirror pairs): a run between two shafts with a hole down in it is an OPEN CHAMBER round the hole; one without is the two tunnels. On the moon a slope is walked down from its own frame (`mcRampSnap`: `ctfRampSnap` read Capture the Flag's index, which is the last CTF map's; the guest's prediction steps up as on CTF and does not snap, as on CTF), and the CPUs walk the slopes (`mcGapHop`: a slope's next step up or down is floor at its own level; `mcRockHop`: never hopped), wait to be out from under a tunnel's roof before jumping for a shaft's first step (`mcRoofOver`), and come out of a hole's top step on their target's side (`R.tx`). Every piece is centred on its run or its half: the halves still mirror exactly, every size. WHAT THE CLIMB TEST FOUND (a CPU from the furnace's floor to the surface, six starts): with the lower tunnel 150 px high, three climbs in six at 10 a side never got out in a minute -- out of a hole into the lower tunnel, a CPU's double jump over the next hole in its floor bonked the roof and it fell back to the furnace, again and again; and with a hole down between every two shafts no run was without one. Hence the 200 px, and every other hole with its chamber. The test itself placed a foe over a hole in the crust: it fell onto the hole's top step, the climber was on its layer then and never went up (that start's 58 s on main was the same) -- the foes now stand clear of the holes. Every climb gets out: 5 a side 14-40 s (main 14-22), 10 a side 16-39 s (main 20-31). THE CPU PROBE (CPU-only, 3 minutes, items; 5 a side seeds 7 and 3, 10 a side seeds 7, 3, 11 and 5): nobody stuck, no quiet falls into the core, no errors; KOs 20 at 5 a side (the layers' build 14, live main before the layers 20) and 47 at 10 a side (main 43) -- the KOs the layers cost are back; near a foe 0.66 / 0.68 at 5 a side (main 0.60 / 0.64) and 0.41-0.56 at 10 (mean 0.48; main 0.47-0.51, mean 0.49). The longest a CPU went without a foe within 300 px: 25-49 s at 5 a side (main 41-64) and 54-180 s at 10 (main 76-101) -- the 180 s Snowball circling its own strip on the surface and in the galleries the whole match, no tunnel near it: the old loitering main shows at 75-100 s. THE GATES: the replay of the 11 recorded matches on the other maps is identical to main's (Capture the Flag's among them: its slopes are as they were); online, a host and a guest at 5 a side in three spots build the same disc, no errors, and the guest's corrections are main's or smaller (mean 3.4 / 5.5 / 2.6 px, main 3.4 / 7.0 / 5.4; none over 160 px, main three); a step at 30 CPUs is 24.3 / 23.8 ms in jsdom against main's 23.6 / 23.5 (2% more; the 95th percentile 37-39 against 34). Pictures (headless Chromium): the tunnels at 5, 10 and 15 a side, in the lower tunnel and on the upper one's hills.

357 s, Get Digging 59 s of game; his gush, Soak Up, finisher and Squishy Top in all three; paid 20, 50 and 5 as the rules say), settings handed back exactly each time; the pair (guest Spongy, host without the unlock; WebSocket off, relay.invalid) shows Wring Out (full water: the twist, the gush from the host's source, 12%, soaked), Soak Up, Squash and Spring, Falls From the Sky (its spot, the pin, Pen flattened, 15%) and Cannonballs Were on Sale (wind, jet, chute, the balls, 31%) the same on both screens; his own walk and jump end 0.0 px from the host's; the Sponge Floor (set on both ends) bounces him to the same height (418 / 417); and with the host on Spongy the guest's own Pen springs off his top to the same peak (386.6 / 386.5); Moon Caves (read-only smoke) a 60 s Teams match with a CPU Spongy on the disc ran with no errors (his gush, Soak Up and Final Smash went off); his moves were not checked or changed for the disc (Moon Caves belongs to its own session). (3) Those two cost him: 12 of 30 (seeds 20261028 + 20261103: 7 + 5) became 8 (3 + 5) -- the missed throws had also carried a slow fighter to his foe -- so run 0.95 -> 1.0, the design's second knob again (recovery is at the top of its range): 12 of 30 (7 + 5) again. Her Supervan (`attacks/van.png`, the one her special drives; `vanShape` until it loads) grows in over 12 frames on the spot she stands; she hops into it (18 frames, a 54 px arc to her seat 26 px ahead of her; inside it she is not drawn: `hides`, from the wire fields `fsT`/`fsC`/`fsWind`); its exhaust pipe puffs smoke (one puff every 3 frames, every 2 over the last 14) while the van shakes harder as it builds (30 frames); the catch lands on row frame 48 and the blast goes at frame 62, 14 frames later (`FS_WIND`, as before). The blast is the old one, centred on the van's middle (where she stood: `fsAx`/`fsAy`, so it reaches what it reached): inner ring 300 px at 20%, outer 420 px at 14% for whoever the first did not reach, 14 frames apart (40% and 28% after `FS_DMG_MULT`), the same launches (20/-13 and 18/-12). The van blows apart with it and throws her out: a scripted 26-frame arc (76 px high) of up to 240 px, away from the foes the blast reaches (none, or dead centre: backwards), facing the blast, down on the floor or platform she stood on (`fsPencilRoom`: it stops 28 px inside the surface's edge or a solid block, and if the way away from the foes has under 70 px of room and the other way has 60 more, she goes the other way; in the air, any surface below counts; none on the Moon). Untouchable the whole way, as every holder is (`invuln` each frame). The row is 144 frames now (was 96). Framework, two row options and nothing else: `catchAt` (the row frame the catch lands on, in `fsRun`; without it the catch lands first as always) so nobody is held through the 48 entrance frames -- the hold is 17 frames, main's was 18 -- and `hides(f)` (the holder is not drawn, with her aura and tag: `fsHides` in `draw()`). No new wire fields (`fsBx`/`fsBy` hold her seat, which is also which way the van faces). The Final Smash has no line on any card or in the move list, so there is no card text to change; the row's comment carries the description. THE SEQUENCE (row frames, after the 30-frame intro and the 10-frame catch; the foe is held from the catch, in the air if they were): frames 0-13 a chair comes down over the foe from above, grown in from small over 12 frames, and clamps round them like a cage, a leg either side, with a squash and dust at 13 (no damage: it only holds them; it then goes see-through, jolts at each blow and lifts away with the launch); then EIGHT chairs, 14 frames apart, landing on frames 28, 42, 56, 70, 84, 98, 112, 126, each swung in clockwise over 12 frames growing in from small, her own chair drawing (`PROP_ART.chair`), legs toward the foe, hitting and going on round and out: from the lower left, left, upper left, above, upper right, right, lower right, and the eighth from straight below. Each chair deals exactly 12.5% (the row's 6.25 doubled by KO-LEVEL, `FS_DMG_MULT`; 100% in all) through `applyHit` with Bow as the attacker, so kills credit her. The eighth's launch is up (-28 and 6 sideways away from her, KO-level through `fsKbMult`) and is let go three frames after it by the catch's own let-go; the first seven stun only 4 frames, so the eighth is no link of a chain and its let-go stuns in full and the slow-mo sees the launch. 140 frames in all. She hangs where she is. WHAT THE EIGHTH KOs AT, from the foe's damage before the Final Smash (the foe a CPU from the let-go, steering back; starts at 0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 60, 80%): on Goiky Field and Pillars from 0% for a light (Puffball) and a mid-weight (Pen) fighter, from 10% for the heaviest (Tree); on the big Sky Tower Puffball from 0%, Pen from 10%, Tree from 30%; it cannot carry a foe out from under a solid block (Yoyleland's middle one). FIZZLE: pressed with no foe below her in reach it is spent with a small puff and nothing else, no intro and no pause (row flags `needFoe`; `fsFizzle`); if the foe steps out in the catch's 10 frames after the intro it ends there with the same puff. A CPU never throws it away: the row's `exact` flag drops the "glow nearly out and a foe somewhere near" fire, its `botOk` wants the foe clearly below (14 px to spare) and where the catch will find them 10 frames on at the speed they go, and a CPU press that arrives with nobody below (its keys lag) is swallowed and the glow kept (`fsIsCpu`); in four CPU free-for-alls with Bow 25 of 27 casts caught their foe (the two misses: a foe who stepped out in the catch's 10 frames). INTERRUPTS end it clean (the row checks the foe, `koSince`, before every frame; nothing is thrown at whoever comes back; no lock outlives it; one stock, never two): a damage-cap KO on a chair, the target KO'd another way, a Bubble's pop, Bow KO'd by a third party, a third fighter's Final Smash going off mid-sequence, the launch that ends a match. Online nothing new is on the wire: a guest draws it all from `fsT`, `fsLk` and the foe's spot and size in the existing `fsBx`, `fsBy`, `fsCx`, the same on both screens (the in-process pair). NOT checked (the owner's call): the CPU probe, online, the replay of the other maps and the speed -- the pieces at 15 a side are 570 against 296, so a step there is likely slower than before.

### O31 · Event fighters: kit 4 (Black Hole) -- what it left  [BUILT on `g-ev-blackhole`, NOT PUSHED: two decisions for you first]

Kit 4 (branch `g-ev-blackhole`, build step 11 of event-fighters-phase1.md) is Black Hole (he/him), from section 3.4 and the approved
questions 43 to 49. He is the fourth event row in ROSTER (`event:true`, kit `accrete`). His code is the section EVENT FIGHTERS: BLACK HOLE
(`BLACK_HOLE`, the `bh*` functions): Tug (jab), the tilts, the five aerials, the grab and four throws, Accretion / Slingshot, Wormhole,
Singularity, Event Horizon (SMASH_SPEC `horizon`), Spaghettify (FINX `spag`) and Mass of Twenty Suns (`FINAL_SMASHES['Black Hole']`). The Death
P.A.C.T. rule (`bhNoDown`): none of his hits sends a foe downward. His hurtbox is his black middle only (HURTBOX's circle, question 47).
His side special (Gravity Slide) stays off, kept as a data row. His assist row left the bag (`ASSIST_LEAVES_AT_LAUNCH`). His three event
matches are data (`EVENT_MATCHES['Black Hole']`), with two new twists, the pull and low gravity anywhere (`EVENT_TWISTS_BUILT`: `pull`,
`lowgrav`). Art: no downloads; his one render (already in the game as his assist picture), scaled, spun, squashed and stretched; the clod
and the pull streaks are drawn in code (CREDITS.md, `scripts/sprite-manifest.json`). Online: everything he draws rides the snapshot.
Contact sheet: `smash-bal/reports/moves/event-blackhole-sheet.png`.

- **Picks the design left open (the builder's own, not the owner's):**
  - *Death P.A.C.T. Again* (the 3 vs 1): the Teams arena with Yoyle Canyon's sky, items on (his Accretion pulls in loose items), no Smash Balls.
  - *Gravity* (the duel): Goiky Field, items off, no Smash Balls.
  - *Back to the Top*: Sky Towers in low gravity, items on, with Smash Balls.
- **Fixed after the check (2026-11-01):**
  - Spaghettify on the floor: the foe slid along the floor straight through his core. Now near a floor the low half of the orbit is turned
    over his head (`spU`), so they swing over him and back. In the air the full circle stays.
  - Singularity also spent the shared special cooldown, so it locked his Wormhole for 51 frames. Now it has only its own cooldown (150), as
    its comment said. The Wormhole fires the frame the Singularity ends.
  - The pull twist: `PULL.max` 1.2 -> 1.0. The old number left out HEAVIES MOVE SLOWER. The slowest walk is Tree's, 4.85 px a frame, and a
    quarter of it is 1.21, so 1.2 had almost no room.
  - Event Horizon's pull 10 -> 7 (the nerf rule of 2026-10-26). The design's 220 px and 21% are unchanged. A foe running away now gets clear
    from about 150 px; before, it was reeled in from about 180 px.
  - `scripts/trace-hurtboxes.mjs` skips him, so a re-trace cannot replace his middle-only circle with his whole render.
- **Balance: still well above the middle of the pack (YOUR DECISION; accepted at the strong end on 2026-11-02).** The tournament: 15 1v1 matches against 15 mid-pack Hard CPUs, run
  on three seed sets.
  - Before the fixes: 10, 12 and 15 wins out of 15, so 37 of 45.
  - After them: 14, 11 and 13, so 38 of 45.
  - Event Horizon still scores the most KOs (median KO about 120%).
  - Nerfs measured but not kept, each on two seed sets (30 matches):
    - pull 7 with 18%: 12 wins on one set;
    - reach 150, pull 7 and 18%: 23 of 30 (Spaghettify then took most KOs);
    - weaker launches on Event Horizon and Spaghettify: 27 of 30;
    - his hurtbox at the normal 24 (question 47's answer reversed, as a test only): 21 of 30.
  - No single Event Horizon change brings him to the middle: he is strong all round. The normal-size hurtbox gave the lowest number, but
    15 matches a set are noisy (one set went 10 -> 14 after a nerf). Choose the nerf.
- **OWNER DECISION BEFORE THIS IS PUSHED: how a kit joins the lap (O28's open question).** `eventLapOrder` runs the lap only through the
  kits that are built. So his row alone RE-DEALS EVERY WEEK SINCE THE LAUNCH: Eraser 10-26, Black Hole 11-02, Eraser 11-09, Black Hole 11-16,
  and so on. Today every week is Eraser's. Next week's card would change at once, against "a new lap is announced at least two weeks ahead".
  Spongy and 8-Ball come before him in the lap, so each of them landing would re-deal the weeks again. Two ways out:
  - O28's rule: each kit joins on a Monday of its own, at least two weeks after its build goes live;
  - or merge the kits strictly in lap order (Spongy, 8-Ball, Black Hole), with your OK.
  - **DECIDED 2026-11-02: "Straight into the lap, now"** (see O28): all six kits join the lap with their rows, and the week in progress re-deals.
- **Before merging:** the assist bag. Live main has 16 assists (Eraser left), and his leaving makes 15. The design says never below 17. Assist
  batch A2 (`g-ev-a2`, +7) is built but not merged. Merge it first, or together with this kit.
  **DONE 2026-11-02 (`g-ev-integrate`): A2 is merged in the same build, and with the five kits' launch departures the bag holds 18 (floor 17).**
- **Moon Caves:** nothing of his was changed for it, and it was not touched. A read-only check on the moon found no errors.
